# Plan: Comprehensive App Testing & Bug Fixing — Size Chart & Recommender

## Context
The app builds cleanly (zero TS errors, zero lint errors) but has **zero tests**. Before production launch, we need to validate all server logic and public API endpoints work correctly, fix known issues, and ensure the app is production-grade. Since this is a Shopify embedded app, admin UI routes require OAuth (can't be browser-tested without a live dev store session), so we focus on **unit tests** for all server logic and **integration tests** for all 4 public API endpoints.

## Known Issues to Fix

| # | Issue | File | Severity |
|---|-------|------|----------|
| 1 | Import style inconsistency (`~/` vs `../`) | `app/routes/api.recommend.tsx` lines 2-7 | Low |
| 2 | Missing `.env.example` | Root directory | Medium |
| 3 | `shopify.app.toml` has `example.com` placeholder | `shopify.app.toml` | Medium (deploy blocker) |
| 4 | No test infrastructure | Project-wide | High |

---

## Phase 1: Install Test Infrastructure

### Step 1.1 — Install Vitest
```bash
npm install -D vitest @vitest/coverage-v8
```
Why Vitest: shares Vite config, understands TS path aliases natively, fast, ESM-native.

### Step 1.2 — Create `vitest.config.ts`
```ts
import { defineConfig } from "vitest/config";
import tsconfigPaths from "vite-tsconfig-paths";

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [tsconfigPaths()],
  test: {
    globals: true,
    environment: "node",
    include: ["tests/**/*.test.ts"],
    coverage: { provider: "v8", reporter: ["text", "html"] },
  },
});
```

### Step 1.3 — Add scripts to `package.json`
```json
"test": "vitest run",
"test:watch": "vitest",
"test:coverage": "vitest run --coverage"
```

### Step 1.4 — Create test directory structure
```
tests/
  unit/
    ai.server.test.ts
    plans.test.ts
    api-utils.test.ts
    rate-limit.test.ts
    templates.test.ts
  integration/
    api-size-chart.test.ts
    api-recommend.test.ts
    api-widget-config.test.ts
    api-analytics-event.test.ts
    setup.ts              (shared test DB seeding + server helpers)
```

---

## Phase 2: Unit Tests (~50 test cases)

### `tests/unit/plans.test.ts` — Plan Logic
- `getPlan("free")` returns Free plan
- `getPlan("unknown")` falls back to Free
- `hasFeature("free", "analytics")` returns false
- `hasFeature("growth", "analytics")` returns true
- `hasFeature("pro", "advancedAi")` returns true
- `canCreateChart("free", 2)` returns true (under limit of 3)
- `canCreateChart("free", 3)` returns false (at limit)
- `canCreateChart("starter", 999)` returns true (unlimited = -1)
- All 4 plans have required fields (id, name, price, limits)

**Reuses**: `getPlan`, `hasFeature`, `canCreateChart` from `app/lib/plans.ts`

### `tests/unit/api-utils.test.ts` — CORS, Shop Validation, CSS Sanitization
- `validateShop("test-store.myshopify.com")` returns null (valid)
- `validateShop(null)` returns 400 response
- `validateShop("")` returns 400 response
- `validateShop("-bad.myshopify.com")` returns 400 (starts with hyphen)
- `validateShop("store.notshopify.com")` returns 400
- `validateShop("store.myshopify.com.evil.com")` returns 400
- `corsJson({ok:true})` includes all CORS headers
- `corsJson({}, {status:404})` has correct status
- `corsOptions()` returns 204 with CORS headers
- `sanitizeCss("body{color:red}")` passes through
- `sanitizeCss("@import url(...)")` blocks import
- `sanitizeCss("url(javascript:alert(1))")` blocks javascript:
- `sanitizeCss("url(data:text/html,...)")` blocks data:text/html
- `sanitizeCss("expression(alert(1))")` blocks expression
- `sanitizeCss("behavior:url(xss)")` blocks behavior
- `sanitizeCss("<script>")` blocks script tags
- `sanitizeCss("")` returns empty string

**Reuses**: `validateShop`, `corsJson`, `corsOptions`, `sanitizeCss`, `SHOP_RE` from `app/lib/api.server.ts`

### `tests/unit/rate-limit.test.ts` — Rate Limiter
- Allows requests under limit
- Returns null for request #1
- Returns null for request at exactly maxRequests
- Returns 429 Response for request exceeding maxRequests
- 429 response has Retry-After header
- 429 response has CORS headers
- Different keys are isolated (key A at limit doesn't block key B)
- Window reset: after windowMs elapses, count resets (use `vi.useFakeTimers()`)

**Reuses**: `checkRateLimit` from `app/lib/rate-limit.server.ts`

### `tests/unit/ai.server.test.ts` — AI Recommendation Engine
- `estimateMeasurements(175, 75, "male", "regular")` returns valid measurements
- All measurement keys present (chest, waist, hips, shoulder, armLength, inseam, height)
- Male measurements differ from female at same height/weight
- "slim" body type produces smaller chest than "plus"
- "athletic" body type: smaller waist, larger shoulders than "regular"
- BMI extremes: very low weight produces smaller measurements
- `recommendSize` with valid chart returns a size string
- `recommendSize` with empty rows returns empty string + confidence 0
- `recommendSize` with empty headers returns empty string + confidence 0
- Confidence is 0-100 range
- Alternatives filtered to confidence > 5%
- `parseMeasurementValue` (via integration): handles "34", "32-34", "34 cm", "34.5", "", "-"
- Size column detection finds "Size" header

**Reuses**: `estimateMeasurements`, `recommendSize` from `app/lib/ai.server.ts`

### `tests/unit/templates.test.ts` — Size Chart Templates
- Templates array is non-empty
- Each template has required fields (id, name, category, headers, rows)
- Each template has at least 1 row of data
- Headers array is non-empty for each template
- Categories include expected values (tops, bottoms, shoes, etc.)
- Template IDs are unique

**Reuses**: `TEMPLATES` from `app/lib/templates.ts` (need to verify export name)

# Koda Execution Plan: Pinzo Product/Collection-Based Delivery Rules

## Pre-Build Findings

### Codebase State
- Schema is PostgreSQL (not SQLite) — `npx prisma db push` is correct approach since schema has already drifted
- `unauthenticated` is already exported from `app/shopify.server.ts` (line 120) — no change needed there
- `session.scope` exists on the Session model as `String?` — scope checking works
- The widget's `checkZip()` function builds `zipCheckUrl` on line 738 — that is the exact insertion point for `&product=` param
- The container div for the live widget is at lines 97–104 of the liquid file — that is where `data-product-id` goes
- `PLAN_LIMITS` currently has 12 fields in `PlanLimits` — adding `productCollectionRules: boolean` is additive
- Both webhook handlers already have the pattern for cleanup — just need `productCollectionCache` added to the `Promise.all` arrays
- App Bridge `useAppBridge()` is already imported and used in `app.delivery-rules.tsx` (line 228)

### Key Decisions Confirmed
1. Use `npx prisma db push` (not `migrate dev`) — schema is drifted, CLAUDE.md warns about this
2. `unauthenticated.admin(shop)` for the public ZIP check API — already exported, confirmed safe
3. Comma-separated string storage for productIds/collectionIds — consistent with existing `zipCodes` and `daysOfWeek` pattern
4. The `Rule` type in `app.delivery-rules.tsx` (line 207) needs 3 new fields added

---

- Clean up stale old entries for the product
- Return `[]` on any error (graceful degradation)

- Update `handleZipCheck` signature to accept `productId?: string | null`
- Extract `product` param in both `loader` (GET) and `action` (POST) handlers
- Add `matchesZipOrZone` helper function
- Implement 4-tier matching replacing the existing 2-pass logic:
  - Pass 1: product-specific (`targetType === "products"`, productIds contains productId)
  - Pass 2: collection-specific (only if `hasCollectionRules`, calls `getProductCollections`)
  - Pass 3: explicit zip match on `targetType === "all"` rules (existing Pass 1 logic)
  - Pass 4: zone match on `targetType === "all"` rules (existing Pass 2 logic)
- Backward compatibility: when no `productId` param, skips passes 1 and 2 entirely
- Import `getProductCollections` from `../utils/product-collections.server`
- The `handleZipCheck` function must remain callable from both GET loader and POST action
- For POST action: also extract `product` from JSON body for parity

**Important:** The fallback URLs in `doZipFetch` in the widget also need updating but that happens in Step 8 (liquid file). The API-side is self-contained.

Verify build passes before touching the UI.

```typescript
const [targetType, setTargetType] = useState<"all" | "products" | "collections">("all");
const [selectedProductIds, setSelectedProductIds] = useState<string[]>([]);
const [selectedProductNames, setSelectedProductNames] = useState<Record<string, string>>({});
const [selectedCollectionIds, setSelectedCollectionIds] = useState<string[]>([]);
const [selectedCollectionNames, setSelectedCollectionNames] = useState<Record<string, string>>({});
```

- Pass new fields into `data` object for create/update

**7h. Add Resource Picker handlers:**
```typescript
const handleSelectProducts = async () => {
  const selection = await shopify.resourcePicker({
    type: "product",
    multiple: true,
    selectionIds: selectedProductIds.map(id => ({ id: `gid://shopify/Product/${id}` })),
  });
  if (selection) {
    const ids = selection.map(p => p.id.split("/").pop()).filter(Boolean) as string[];
    const names: Record<string, string> = {};
    selection.forEach(p => { const id = p.id.split("/").pop(); if (id) names[id] = p.title; });
    setSelectedProductIds(ids);
    setSelectedProductNames(names);
  }
};
// Same pattern for collections
```

**7i. Add "Product Targeting" section in modal (between Rule Name and Zone fields):**
- Check `!hasProductScope`: show Banner (warning tone) explaining permissions needed
- Three radio-button-style UI using Polaris `ChoiceList` component with choices: "All Products", "Specific Products", "Specific Collections"
- When "Specific Products": show `Button` "Select Products" + list of selected products with `Tag` components and remove buttons
- When "Specific Collections": same pattern for collections
- If `!limits.productCollectionRules`: show the ChoiceList but disable the "Specific Products" and "Specific Collections" options, add a Badge "Pro+" next to disabled choices
- If `!hasProductScope`: disable the non-"all" choices

**7j. Update IndexTable headings:** Add "Target" column after "Rule Name"

**7k. Update IndexTable row:** Add `<IndexTable.Cell>` showing target display text:
- `targetType === "all"`: `<Badge>All Products</Badge>`
- `targetType === "products"`: `"N product(s)"` using `productIds?.split(",").length`
- `targetType === "collections"`: `"N collection(s)"`

---

## TypeScript Pitfalls to Watch

1. `resourcePicker` return type — App Bridge v4 types: `selection` is `Resource[] | undefined`. Each item has `id: string` and `title: string`. Use `as string` casts carefully.

2. `admin.graphql` response type — use `const data = await response.json() as { data: { nodes: Array<{ id: string; title: string } | null> } }` — nodes can contain nulls for deleted items.

3. New fields in `Rule` type — after adding `targetType`, `productIds`, `collectionIds`, make sure `openEdit` and `resetForm` and the table row all reference these typed fields.

4. `unauthenticated.admin(shop)` in `product-collections.server.ts` — this returns `{ admin }` where `admin.graphql` is the typed GraphQL client. Same API as `authenticate.admin`.

5. `PlanLimits` interface change in `plans.ts` — after adding `productCollectionRules`, TypeScript will error on every place that constructs a `PlanLimits` object (i.e., all 4 tier configs) until they all have the new field. Fix all 4 simultaneously.

---

3. Run `npm run build` to verify production build passes

## Risk Notes
- CSV import `bulk-import` action still reads cols[3] (message), cols[4] (eta), cols[5] (COD), cols[6] (returnPolicy) from the CSV and writes them to DB — this is intentional backward compat, leave it alone
- `range-import` action currently reads `message` and `eta` from formData and uses them in DB writes — since we're removing those fields from the Range Import modal, we also remove the `fd.set()` calls from `handleRangeImport`. The action itself can still accept them (no harm if they're missing — they'll be null). We do NOT touch the action's server-side parsing.
- The `handleRangeImport` `rangeMessage`/`rangeEta` state removal also requires removing them from the Range modal's `onClose` handler and the modal's `secondaryActions` cancel handler.
# Architecture Plan: Pinzo Product/Collection-Based Delivery Rules

## Stack
Stack B: React Router 7 (`@shopify/shopify-app-react-router` v1) + Polaris v13 + Prisma v6 + PostgreSQL + App Bridge v4

No stack change. This is a feature addition to the existing Pinzo app.

---

## 1. Data Model Changes

### Approach: Comma-separated Shopify numeric IDs on DeliveryRule

**Why comma-separated strings (like `zipCodes`) instead of JSON or relation tables:**
- Consistent with existing pattern: `zipCodes` and `daysOfWeek` already use comma-separated strings
- Simpler Prisma queries: no joins, no JSON parsing at query time
- Acceptable scale: merchants typically have 10-50 products per rule, not thousands
- Easier form serialization: FormData naturally handles strings

**Why numeric IDs (not full GIDs):**
- Full GIDs (`gid://shopify/Product/123456`) are verbose and waste storage
- The numeric portion is unique within a shop context
- Widget on storefront has access to `{{ product.id }}` which is the numeric ID
- Conversion: `gid://shopify/Product/123456` <-> `123456` is trivial
- Store as strings (not integers) because Shopify IDs can be very large numbers

### Schema Changes to `DeliveryRule`

```prisma
model DeliveryRule {
  // ... existing fields unchanged ...
  
  // NEW: Product/Collection targeting
  targetType    String   @default("all")  // "all" | "products" | "collections"
  productIds    String?  // comma-separated numeric Shopify product IDs (e.g., "123456,789012")
  collectionIds String?  // comma-separated numeric Shopify collection IDs (e.g., "111,222")
  
  // ... existing fields continue ...
}
```

**Field semantics:**
- `targetType = "all"`: Rule applies to all products (current behavior, backward compatible)
- `targetType = "products"`: Rule applies only to products listed in `productIds`
- `targetType = "collections"`: Rule applies only to products in collections listed in `collectionIds`
- A rule can target products OR collections, not both simultaneously (keeps logic clean, avoids ambiguity)
- `productIds` and `collectionIds` are null when not applicable

**Default value `"all"` ensures backward compatibility**: all existing rules continue working without migration beyond adding the column.

### New Model: `ProductCollectionCache`

```prisma
model ProductCollectionCache {
  id            String   @id @default(cuid())
  shop          String
  productId     String   // numeric Shopify product ID (as string)
  collectionId  String   // numeric Shopify collection ID (as string)
  cachedAt      DateTime @default(now())

  @@unique([shop, productId, collectionId])
  @@index([shop, productId])
  @@index([shop, collectionId])
  @@index([cachedAt])  // for TTL-based cleanup
}
```

**Why cache product-collection membership in DB:**
- The ZIP check API is a public endpoint called on every product page visit
- Looking up "which collections does product X belong to?" requires a Shopify Admin API call
- Shopify Admin API has rate limits (40 points/second for REST, 1000 points/second for GraphQL)
- A busy store could generate hundreds of ZIP checks per minute -- hitting Shopify API every time is not viable
- DB lookup: ~1-5ms. Shopify API call: ~200-500ms + rate limit risk.

**Cache invalidation strategy:**
- TTL-based: cache entries older than 6 hours are considered stale
- On API check: if cache miss for a product, do a single Shopify API call, cache the result
- Background refresh: optional webhook-based sync (see Section 2 below)
- Manual purge: admin can trigger a re-sync from the delivery rules page

### Migration Plan

```
npx prisma migrate dev --name add-product-collection-targeting
```

This is a purely additive migration:
- 3 new columns on `DeliveryRule` (all with defaults or nullable)
- 1 new table `ProductCollectionCache`
- No data migration needed: existing rules default to `targetType = "all"`

### GDPR / Cleanup

Add `ProductCollectionCache` to both cleanup handlers:
- `webhooks.app.uninstalled.tsx`: `db.productCollectionCache.deleteMany({ where: { shop } })`
- `webhooks.shop.redact.tsx`: same

---

## 2. API Scope Changes

### Current scopes
```toml
scopes = "read_themes"
```

### Required new scope
```toml
scopes = "read_themes,read_products"
```

**Why `read_products`:**
- Resource Picker (App Bridge) requires `read_products` to browse products/collections
- Collection membership lookup (`product.collections`) requires product read access
- No write access needed -- we never modify products

### Scope Migration for Existing Installs

**Shopify handles this automatically via `use_legacy_install_flow = false`:**
1. Update `shopify.app.toml` with new scopes
2. Run `shopify app deploy`
3. Existing merchants see a "permissions updated" banner in Shopify Admin when they next open the app
4. They click "Approve" to grant the new scope
5. Until approved, the app continues working with old scopes (all existing features unaffected)

**Graceful degradation in code:**
- Before showing the Resource Picker, check if `read_products` scope is granted
- If not granted, show a Polaris `Banner` explaining that the merchant needs to approve new permissions
- Link to the OAuth re-auth flow
- The `session.scope` field in the Session model contains the granted scopes as a comma-separated string

**Implementation:**
```typescript
// In the delivery rules loader
const hasProductScope = session.scope?.includes("read_products") ?? false;
// Pass to component; conditionally render product/collection picker
```

### Webhook for Collection Updates (Optional, Phase 2)

Register `collections/update` and `products/update` webhooks to invalidate the `ProductCollectionCache`:
```toml
[[webhooks.subscriptions]]
topics = [ "collections/update" ]
uri = "/webhooks/collections/update"

[[webhooks.subscriptions]]
topics = [ "products/update" ]  
uri = "/webhooks/products/update"
```

**For V1, skip webhooks.** The 6-hour TTL cache is sufficient. Merchants rarely reorganize collections, and when they do, the cache self-corrects within hours. Add webhooks in V2 if merchants report stale data.

---

## 3. Storefront Widget Changes

### Passing Product ID to the Widget

The Liquid block already renders inside the product page template. Shopify's Liquid context provides `product.id` on product pages.

**Change to `zip-code-checker.liquid`:**

```liquid
<div
  id="zcc-widget-{{ block.id }}"
  data-zcc-widget="true"
  data-shop="{{ shop.permanent_domain }}"
  data-api="{{ app.url }}"
  data-proxy="/apps/zipcheck"
  data-block="{{ block.id }}"
  {% if product %}data-product-id="{{ product.id }}"{% endif %}
  {% if collection %}data-collection-id="{{ collection.id }}"{% endif %}
  {{ block.shopify_attributes }}
></div>
```

**Key points:**
- `product.id` is the numeric Shopify product ID (e.g., `123456`), exactly what we store
- `{% if product %}` ensures the attribute is only added on product pages, not cart pages or standalone pages
- `collection.id` is added for collection pages -- useful for collection-specific rules
- On pages where neither is available (homepage, cart), neither attribute is set, and the API falls back to general rules

**JS widget change (in `init()` function):**

```javascript
var PRODUCT_ID = container.dataset.productId || null;
var COLLECTION_ID = container.dataset.collectionId || null;

// In the zip check URL construction:
var zipCheckUrl = API_BASE + "/api/zip-check?shop=" + encodeURIComponent(SHOP) 
  + "&zip=" + encodeURIComponent(zip);
if (PRODUCT_ID) zipCheckUrl += "&product=" + encodeURIComponent(PRODUCT_ID);
if (COLLECTION_ID) zipCheckUrl += "&collection=" + encodeURIComponent(COLLECTION_ID);
```

### Collection Pages

On collection pages (`/collections/xyz`), Shopify provides `collection.id` but not `product.id`. The widget can pass the collection ID so the API returns collection-specific delivery rules.

However, on a collection page the widget is typically not useful (no single product context). Two approaches:

**Recommended (V1):** Only pass `product.id` on product pages. Collection-based rules are resolved server-side by looking up the product's collections. The `data-collection-id` attribute is a bonus for future use but not required for rule matching.

**Why:** A customer checks delivery for a specific product. Even if the rule targets a collection, the API resolves it by checking if the product belongs to that collection. The collection page context is secondary.

### Widget on Non-Product Pages

On pages without product context (homepage, custom pages), the widget sends no `product` param. The API returns only general rules (`targetType = "all"`). This is correct behavior.

---

## 4. ZIP Check API Changes

### New Matching Priority (4-tier)

```
1. Product-specific rules (targetType = "products", productIds contains this product)
2. Collection-specific rules (targetType = "collections", product belongs to collection)
3. Zone-based general rules (targetType = "all", zone or zipCodes match)
4. Default general rules (targetType = "all", no zone filter)
```

Within each tier, rules are still sorted by `priority ASC` (lower = higher priority).

### Updated API Signature

```
GET /api/zip-check?shop=X&zip=Y&product=Z
```

- `product` (optional): numeric Shopify product ID
- `collection` (optional): numeric Shopify collection ID (fallback context, lower priority than product)

### Implementation Logic

```typescript
async function handleZipCheck(shop: string, zip: string, productId?: string) {
  // ... existing zip record lookup unchanged ...
  
  // Fetch all active rules for this shop
  const activeRules = await db.deliveryRule.findMany({
    where: { shop, isActive: true },
    orderBy: { priority: "asc" },
  });
  
  let matchedRule = null;
  
  if (productId) {
    // PASS 1: Product-specific rules
    for (const rule of activeRules) {
      if (rule.targetType === "products" && rule.productIds) {
        const ids = rule.productIds.split(",").map(id => id.trim());
        if (ids.includes(productId)) {
          if (matchesZipOrZone(rule, normalizedZip, zipZone)) {
            matchedRule = rule;
            break;
          }
        }
      }
    }
    
    // PASS 2: Collection-specific rules
    if (!matchedRule) {
      const productCollections = await getProductCollections(shop, productId);
      for (const rule of activeRules) {
        if (rule.targetType === "collections" && rule.collectionIds) {
          const ruleCollectionIds = rule.collectionIds.split(",").map(id => id.trim());
          if (ruleCollectionIds.some(cid => productCollections.includes(cid))) {
            if (matchesZipOrZone(rule, normalizedZip, zipZone)) {
              matchedRule = rule;
              break;
            }
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
  
  // PASS 3: Explicit zip match on general rules (existing logic)
  if (!matchedRule) {
    for (const rule of activeRules) {
      if (rule.targetType === "all" && rule.zipCodes) {
        const zips = rule.zipCodes.split(",").map(z => z.trim().toUpperCase());
        if (zips.includes(normalizedZip)) {
          matchedRule = rule;
          break;
        }
      }
    }
  }
  
  // PASS 4: Zone match on general rules (existing logic)
  if (!matchedRule && zipZone) {
    for (const rule of activeRules) {
      if (rule.targetType === "all" && rule.zone === zipZone && !rule.zipCodes?.trim()) {
        matchedRule = rule;
        break;
      }
    }
  }
  
  // ... rest of response construction unchanged ...
}
```

### Helper: `matchesZipOrZone`

Product/collection rules still need to be constrained by geography. A rule targeting "Product X in Zone A" should not match if the customer's ZIP is in Zone B.

```typescript
function matchesZipOrZone(
  rule: DeliveryRule, 
  normalizedZip: string, 
  zipZone: string | null
): boolean {
  // If rule has explicit zip codes, check those
  if (rule.zipCodes?.trim()) {
    const zips = rule.zipCodes.split(",").map(z => z.trim().toUpperCase());
    return zips.includes(normalizedZip);
  }
  // If rule has a zone, check zone match
  if (rule.zone) {
    return rule.zone === zipZone;
  }
  // No geographic constraint = matches all zips
  return true;
}
```

**Key design decision:** Product/collection rules can optionally be combined with zone/zip constraints. This lets merchants create rules like "Product X in Manhattan = $5 fee" vs "Product X in Brooklyn = $8 fee". If no zone/zip is specified on a product rule, it applies everywhere.

### Collection Membership Lookup

```typescript
async function getProductCollections(
  shop: string, 
  productId: string
): Promise<string[]> {
  // Check cache first (TTL: 6 hours)
  const sixHoursAgo = new Date(Date.now() - 6 * 60 * 60 * 1000);
  const cached = await db.productCollectionCache.findMany({
    where: {
      shop,
      productId,
      cachedAt: { gt: sixHoursAgo },
    },
    select: { collectionId: true },
  });
  
  if (cached.length > 0) {
    return cached.map(c => c.collectionId);
  }
  
  // Cache miss: fetch from Shopify API
  try {
    const collectionIds = await fetchProductCollectionsFromShopify(shop, productId);
    
    // Store in cache (upsert to handle race conditions)
    await Promise.all(
      collectionIds.map(collectionId =>
        db.productCollectionCache.upsert({
          where: {
            shop_productId_collectionId: { shop, productId, collectionId },
          },
          create: { shop, productId, collectionId },
          update: { cachedAt: new Date() },
        })
      )
    );
    
    // Clean up old entries for this product (they may have been removed from collections)
    await db.productCollectionCache.deleteMany({
      where: {
        shop,
        productId,
        cachedAt: { lt: sixHoursAgo },
      },
    });
    
    return collectionIds;
  } catch (error) {
    console.error("[zip-check] Failed to fetch collections for product", productId, error);
    // On API failure, return empty -- fall through to general rules
    return [];
  }
}
```

### Shopify API Call for Collection Membership

**Problem:** The ZIP check API is a public endpoint (no Shopify session). We cannot use `authenticate.admin(request)` here.

**Solution:** Use `unauthenticated.admin(shop)` to get an offline admin API client. This uses the shop's offline access token stored in the Session table.

```typescript
import { unauthenticated } from "../shopify.server";

async function fetchProductCollectionsFromShopify(
  shop: string, 
  productId: string
): Promise<string[]> {
  const { admin } = await unauthenticated.admin(shop);
  
  const response = await admin.graphql(`
    query ProductCollections($id: ID!) {
      product(id: $id) {
        collections(first: 50) {
          nodes {
            id
          }
        }
      }
    }
  `, {
    variables: {
      id: `gid://shopify/Product/${productId}`,
    },
  });
  
  const data = await response.json();
  const collections = data?.data?.product?.collections?.nodes ?? [];
  
  // Extract numeric IDs from GIDs
  return collections.map((c: { id: string }) => {
    const match = c.id.match(/\/(\d+)$/);
    return match ? match[1] : null;
  }).filter(Boolean) as string[];
}
```

**Rate limit safety:**
- GraphQL costs 1 point for this simple query
- Budget: 1000 points/second
- Even at 100 concurrent ZIP checks, we use 100 points (10% of budget)
- Cache ensures most requests never hit Shopify API
- Graceful degradation: on API failure, skip product/collection rules and fall through to general rules

### Performance Analysis

**Current flow (no product context):**
1. DB: `zipCode.findUnique` (~2ms)
2. DB: `widgetConfig.findUnique` (~2ms)
3. DB: `shopSettings.findUnique` (~2ms)
4. DB: `deliveryRule.findMany` (~3ms)
5. In-memory: rule matching loop (~0.1ms)
Total: ~9ms

**New flow (with product context, cache hit):**
1-4. Same as above (~9ms)
5. DB: `productCollectionCache.findMany` (~2ms) -- only if collection rules exist
6. In-memory: rule matching loop (~0.2ms)
Total: ~11ms (negligible increase)

**New flow (with product context, cache miss):**
1-4. Same as above (~9ms)
5. Shopify GraphQL API call (~300ms)
6. DB: cache write (~3ms)
7. In-memory: rule matching loop (~0.2ms)
Total: ~312ms (first request only, then cached for 6 hours)

**Optimization:** Only call `getProductCollections` if there are active collection-targeted rules. If all rules are `targetType = "all"` or `"products"`, skip the collection lookup entirely.

```typescript
const hasCollectionRules = activeRules.some(r => r.targetType === "collections");
if (!matchedRule && productId && hasCollectionRules) {
  const productCollections = await getProductCollections(shop, productId);
  // ... collection matching ...
}
```

### Resource Picker (App Bridge v4)

App Bridge v4 provides `shopify.resourcePicker()` for native Shopify product/collection selection.

```typescript
const shopify = useAppBridge();

// Open product picker
const handlePickProducts = async () => {
  const selected = await shopify.resourcePicker({
    type: "product",
    multiple: true,
    selectionIds: selectedProductIds.map(id => ({ 
      id: `gid://shopify/Product/${id}` 
    })),
  });
  
  if (selected) {
    const ids = selected.map(p => {
      const match = p.id.match(/\/(\d+)$/);
      return match ? match[1] : null;
    }).filter(Boolean);
    setSelectedProductIds(ids);
  }
};

// Open collection picker
const handlePickCollections = async () => {
  const selected = await shopify.resourcePicker({
    type: "collection",
    multiple: true,
    selectionIds: selectedCollectionIds.map(id => ({ 
      id: `gid://shopify/Collection/${id}` 
    })),
  });
  
  if (selected) {
    const ids = selected.map(c => {
      const match = c.id.match(/\/(\d+)$/);
      return match ? match[1] : null;
    }).filter(Boolean);
    setSelectedCollectionIds(ids);
  }
};
```

### Displaying Selected Items

**Problem:** We store numeric IDs, but need to display product/collection names in the UI.

**Solution:** When loading the delivery rules page, fetch product/collection names from Shopify API for any rules that have `productIds` or `collectionIds`.

```typescript
// In the loader
const rulesWithTargets = rules.filter(r => r.targetType !== "all");
const allProductIds = new Set<string>();
const allCollectionIds = new Set<string>();

for (const rule of rulesWithTargets) {
  if (rule.productIds) {
    rule.productIds.split(",").forEach(id => allProductIds.add(id.trim()));
  }
  if (rule.collectionIds) {
    rule.collectionIds.split(",").forEach(id => allCollectionIds.add(id.trim()));
  }
}

// Batch fetch names from Shopify (one API call with multiple IDs)
let productNames: Record<string, string> = {};
let collectionNames: Record<string, string> = {};

if (allProductIds.size > 0) {
  const productGids = [...allProductIds].map(id => `gid://shopify/Product/${id}`);
  // Use Shopify GraphQL nodes query to batch fetch
  const response = await admin.graphql(`
    query ProductNames($ids: [ID!]!) {
      nodes(ids: $ids) {
        ... on Product {
          id
          title
        }
      }
    }
  `, { variables: { ids: productGids } });
  
  const data = await response.json();
  for (const node of data?.data?.nodes ?? []) {
    if (node?.id && node?.title) {
      const numId = node.id.match(/\/(\d+)$/)?.[1];
      if (numId) productNames[numId] = node.title;
    }
  }
}

// Same pattern for collections
```

### IndexTable Column Update

Add a "Target" column to the rules table:

```
| Rule Name | Target | Zone | Delivery Fee | Min. Order | ETA | Days | Actions |
```

Target column display:
- `targetType = "all"`: Badge showing "All Products"
- `targetType = "products"`: "3 products" (with tooltip listing names)
- `targetType = "collections"`: "2 collections" (with tooltip listing names)

### Form State Additions

```typescript
// New state in the component
const [targetType, setTargetType] = useState<"all" | "products" | "collections">("all");
const [selectedProductIds, setSelectedProductIds] = useState<string[]>([]);
const [selectedCollectionIds, setSelectedCollectionIds] = useState<string[]>([]);
const [selectedProductNames, setSelectedProductNames] = useState<Record<string, string>>({});
const [selectedCollectionNames, setSelectedCollectionNames] = useState<Record<string, string>>({});
```

### Scope Check Banner

If `read_products` scope is not yet granted:

```tsx
{!hasProductScope && (
  <Banner
    tone="warning"
    title="Additional permission required"
    action={{
      content: "Approve permissions",
      url: "/auth/login", // triggers re-auth
    }}
  >
    <Text as="p">
      Product and collection-based delivery rules require access to your store's
      product data. Click "Approve permissions" to enable this feature.
    </Text>
  </Banner>
)}
```

When the scope is missing, the target type selector is disabled with `"all"` selected.

## 6. Plan Gating

### Tier Strategy

| Feature | Free | Starter | Pro | Ultimate |
|---------|------|---------|-----|----------|
| Delivery rules (general) | 0 | 3 | Unlimited | Unlimited |
| Product-targeted rules | 0 | 0 | Unlimited | Unlimited |
| Collection-targeted rules | 0 | 0 | Unlimited | Unlimited |

**Rationale:**
- Free: No delivery rules at all (existing behavior)
- Starter: General rules only (zone/zip targeting). Product/collection targeting is a power feature
- Pro: Full product/collection targeting. This is the upsell driver
- Ultimate: Same as Pro (already unlimited)

**Why not give Starter limited product rules (e.g., 3)?** Partial access creates confusing edge cases ("I can add 3 products but not 4?"). Clean boundary: general rules on Starter, targeted rules on Pro+.

### Plan Limits Update

```typescript
export interface PlanLimits {
  // ... existing fields ...
  productCollectionRules: boolean;  // NEW
}

export const PLAN_LIMITS: Record<PlanTier, PlanLimits> = {
  free: {
    // ... existing ...
    productCollectionRules: false,
  },
  starter: {
    // ... existing ...
    productCollectionRules: false,
  },
  pro: {
    // ... existing ...
    productCollectionRules: true,
  },
  ultimate: {
    // ... existing ...
    productCollectionRules: true,
  },
};
```

### Gating Enforcement Points

1. **Admin UI (create/update action):** If `targetType !== "all"` and `!limits.productCollectionRules`, return error with upgrade CTA
2. **Admin UI (component):** If `!limits.productCollectionRules`, show target type selector as disabled with Pro badge
3. **API (zip-check):** No gating needed at API level -- if a rule exists with product targeting, it was created when the merchant had the right plan. Even if they downgrade, existing rules should still work (graceful degradation, not punitive deletion)

---

## 7. Performance Considerations

### Collection Membership: Cache in DB

**Decision:** Cache in `ProductCollectionCache` table, NOT in-memory and NOT query Shopify API each time.

**Why not in-memory:**
- Railway single instance restarts lose the cache
- Memory growth unbounded as more products are checked
- No sharing if we ever scale to multiple instances

**Why not Shopify API each time:**
- 200-500ms latency per call
- Rate limit risk under load
- Unnecessary for data that changes infrequently

**Why DB cache:**
- Survives restarts
- ~2ms lookup (indexed)
- Shared across requests
- Automatic TTL cleanup via `cachedAt` comparison
- PostgreSQL handles concurrent writes gracefully

### Conflict Resolution: Product in Multiple Collections

**Scenario:** Product X belongs to Collection A (rule: $5 delivery) and Collection B (rule: $8 delivery).

**Resolution:** First matching rule wins, ordered by `priority ASC`.

This is already the established pattern for zone-based rules. Same principle applies:
- Merchant sets priority on each rule
- Lower priority number = higher precedence
- If a merchant wants Collection A's rule to take priority, they give it priority 0 and Collection B priority 1

**No implicit "most specific wins" logic.** Explicit priority is clearer, less magical, and consistent with existing behavior.

### Cache Cleanup Job

Add a periodic cleanup to remove stale cache entries:

```typescript
// In a utility or called periodically
async function cleanupStaleCache() {
  const sixHoursAgo = new Date(Date.now() - 6 * 60 * 60 * 1000);
  await db.productCollectionCache.deleteMany({
    where: { cachedAt: { lt: sixHoursAgo } },
  });
}
```

**Trigger:** Call at the start of `handleZipCheck` with a throttle (max once per 5 minutes) to avoid running on every request. Use the same in-memory throttle pattern as the rate limiter.

---

- `app/routes/api.widget-config.tsx` -- widget config is independent of targeting
- `extensions/zip-code-widget/blocks/cart-validator.liquid` -- cart validation is ZIP-based, not product-based
- `extensions/zip-code-widget/blocks/app-embed.liquid` -- no change needed

## 9. Sprint Plan

1. Update `prisma/schema.prisma` with new fields and model
2. Run migration

6. Update `app/plans.ts` with `productCollectionRules`

   - Add target type radio group

   - Add `data-product-id` attribute

   - Check ZIP on product page in storefront
   - Verify product rule takes priority over general rule
   - Verify fallback to general rules when no product rule matches
3. Run `shopify app deploy` to update scopes

---

## 10. Risks & Mitigations

| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
|------|-----------|--------|------------|
| Existing merchants don't approve new `read_products` scope | Medium | Medium | Graceful degradation: product targeting simply unavailable until approved. All existing features work without new scope. |
| Shopify API rate limits hit during cache-miss storms | Low | Medium | Cache prevents repeated calls. Graceful fallback to general rules on API failure. |
| Product removed from collection but cache still shows membership | Low | Low | 6-hour TTL auto-corrects. V2 adds webhook-based invalidation. |
| Merchant creates conflicting product + collection rules | Medium | Low | Priority system resolves conflicts deterministically. Document in help text. |
| Large product catalogs (10K+ products) slow down Resource Picker | Low | Low | Resource Picker is Shopify's native component with built-in pagination and search. Not our bottleneck. |
| `ProductCollectionCache` table grows unbounded | Low | Medium | TTL-based cleanup runs automatically. Index on `cachedAt` makes deletion fast. |

---

## 11. Open Questions

None. All design decisions are resolved. Ready for Koda to implement.

---

## 12. Technical Decisions Summary

| Decision | Choice | Reasoning |
|----------|--------|-----------|
| Storage format for product/collection IDs | Comma-separated strings | Consistent with existing `zipCodes` and `daysOfWeek` pattern |
| ID format | Numeric strings ("123456") | Matches `{{ product.id }}` in Liquid; shorter than full GIDs |
| Collection membership lookup | DB cache with 6h TTL | Avoids Shopify API on every ZIP check; survives restarts; ~2ms lookup |
| Product vs Collection mutual exclusivity | One target type per rule | Avoids ambiguity; keeps matching logic simple; merchants can create separate rules |
| Geographic constraint on product rules | Optional (zone/zip can be combined) | Enables "Product X in Manhattan = $5" use cases |
| Conflict resolution | Priority-based (lowest number wins) | Consistent with existing behavior; explicit, not magical |
| Plan gating | Pro+ only | Clean boundary; upsell driver; avoids confusing partial limits |
| Scope migration | Shopify automatic re-consent | Standard Shopify pattern; no breaking change for existing merchants |
| Cache invalidation | TTL-only for V1 | Simple, sufficient, no webhook complexity; add webhooks in V2 if needed |
| Widget product context | `{{ product.id }}` Liquid variable | Available on all product pages; numeric ID matches our storage format |
# Widget Customization Page — Tab Restructuring

## Context
The Widget Customization page (`app/routes/app.widget.tsx`) is a very long scrolling page with 5+ settings cards stacked vertically (Layout, Colors, Text Content, Display Options, Custom CSS) plus a sticky Live Preview on the right. Merchants have to scroll extensively to find settings. The goal is to organize settings into **tabs below the Layout section** so merchants can switch between sections without scrolling.

## Current Structure (scrolling page)
```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Page Header: Widget Customization [Save] [Discard]  │
├──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┤
│ LEFT (Settings)      │ RIGHT (Live Preview)         │
│                      │                              │
│ [Layout Card]        │ [State Toggle Tabs]          │
│  Inline/Float/Popup  │ Default|Success|Block|NotFnd │
│                      │                              │
│ [Colors Card]        │ ┌──────────────────────┐     │
│  5 pickers + radius  │ │  Browser Mockup      │     │
│                      │ │  ┌──────────────┐    │     │
│ [Text Content Card]  │ │  │  Widget       │    │     │
│  6 text fields       │ │  │  Preview      │    │     │
│                      │ │  └──────────────┘    │     │
│ [Display Options]    │ └──────────────────────┘     │
│  12 toggles          │                              │
│  Purchase Protection │                              │
│  Waitlist Engagement │                              │
│                      │                              │
│ [Custom CSS Card]    │                              │
│  textarea            │                              │
└──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘
```

## New Structure (tabbed)
```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Page Header: Widget Customization [Save] [Discard]  │
├──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┤
│ LEFT (Settings)      │ RIGHT (Live Preview)         │
│                      │                              │
│ [Layout Card]        │ [State Toggle Tabs]          │
│  Inline/Float/Popup  │ Default|Success|Block|NotFnd │
│                      │                              │
│ [Design|Content|Options|Advanced]  ← Polaris Tabs   │
│                      │ ┌──────────────────────┐     │
│ ┌──────────────────┐ │ │  Browser Mockup      │     │
│ │ Tab content here │ │ │  ┌──────────────┐    │     │
│ │ (one section     │ │ │  │  Widget       │    │     │
│ │  at a time)      │ │ │  │  Preview      │    │     │
│ └──────────────────┘ │ │  └──────────────┘    │     │
│                      │ └──────────────────────┘     │
└──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘
```

## Tab Organization

### Tab 1: "Design" (default)
- **Colors**: 5 color pickers + border radius
- This is what merchants interact with most — quick visual changes

### Tab 2: "Content"
- **Text Content**: Heading, placeholder, button text, success/error/notfound messages
- **Waitlist Labels**: waitlistTitle, waitlistButtonText

### Tab 3: "Display"
- **Delivery Info Toggles**: ETA, zone, cutoff, days, date, countdown, fee, COD, return policy
- **Waitlist & Social**: showWaitlistOnFailure, showSocialProof

### Tab 4: "Advanced"
- **Purchase Protection**: blockCartOnInvalid, blockCheckoutInCart, lockButtonsUntilZipCheck
- **Custom CSS**: textarea with plan gating

## Implementation

### File to modify
- `/Users/yashbaldha/Desktop/Boldteq App/Pinzo/app/routes/app.widget.tsx`

### Changes
1. **Add tab state**: `const [settingsTab, setSettingsTab] = useState(0)`
2. **Add Polaris Tabs**: Below the Layout Card, add `<Tabs tabs={[...]} selected={settingsTab} onSelect={setSettingsTab} />`
3. **Wrap each section in conditional rendering**: Each card group renders only when its tab is active
4. **Keep Layout Card ABOVE tabs**: Layout (position selector) is always visible since it's the primary choice
5. **Keep Live Preview unchanged**: Right column stays sticky, updates in real-time regardless of active tab
6. **Keep all state variables as-is**: No changes to state management, save/discard/reset logic
7. **Keep all existing card content**: Just wrap in tab conditionals, don't restructure the internals

### Tab definitions (Polaris Tabs format)
```tsx
const settingsTabs = [
  { id: "design", content: "Design" },
  { id: "content", content: "Content" },
  { id: "display", content: "Display" },
  { id: "advanced", content: "Advanced" },
];
```

### Conditional rendering pattern
```tsx
<Tabs tabs={settingsTabs} selected={settingsTab} onSelect={setSettingsTab}>
  {settingsTab === 0 && (
    <Box paddingBlockStart="400">
      {/* Colors Card content */}
    </Box>
  )}
  {settingsTab === 1 && (
    <Box paddingBlockStart="400">
      {/* Text Content + Waitlist Labels */}
    </Box>
  )}
  {settingsTab === 2 && (
    <Box paddingBlockStart="400">
      {/* Display Options toggles */}
    </Box>
  )}
  {settingsTab === 3 && (
    <Box paddingBlockStart="400">
      {/* Purchase Protection + Custom CSS */}
    </Box>
  )}
</Tabs>
```

### What stays the same
- All state management (30+ useState hooks)
- Save/discard/reset handlers
- Live Preview (right column, sticky)
- Layout Card (above tabs, always visible)
- All form field components and their onChange handlers
- Plan gating logic
- Error/success banners

## Verification
1. `npx tsc --noEmit` — zero errors
2. `npm run build` — passes
3. All 4 tabs render their content correctly
4. Switching tabs doesn't lose unsaved changes (state persists across tab switches)
5. Live Preview updates in real-time from any tab
6. Save/Discard work from any tab (they save ALL state, not just visible tab)
7. Plan gating badges still show on restricted features
8. Reset to Defaults still resets everything across all tabs
# Rankora UI/UX Bug Scan Plan

## Bugs Found

### BUG 1 — P2: Bare `<Card>` without border/shadow styling (Index.tsx line 359)
File: `src/pages/Index.tsx`
Line 359: `<Card>` — missing `className="border border-border/60 shadow-sm"`
Fix: Add `className="border border-border/60 shadow-sm"` to match all other cards.

### BUG 2 — P2: Bare `<Card>` without border/shadow styling (RequestFeature.tsx line 314)
File: `src/pages/RequestFeature.tsx`
Line 314: `<Card>` — empty state card missing border/shadow
Line 323: `<Card key={r.id} className="border hover:border-border/80 transition-colors">` — missing `shadow-sm`
Fix: Add `className="border border-border/60 shadow-sm"` to line 314, add `shadow-sm` to line 323.

### BUG 3 — P2: Inconsistent padding in RequestFeature.tsx and BookDemo.tsx
Files: `src/pages/RequestFeature.tsx` (line 194), `src/pages/BookDemo.tsx` (line 70)
Both use `mx-auto max-w-5xl px-4 sm:px-6 lg:px-8 py-10` instead of `w-full px-6 sm:px-8 lg:px-10 py-8`.
Fix: Replace with `w-full px-6 sm:px-8 lg:px-10 py-8`.

### BUG 4 — P2: Admin.tsx uses inconsistent padding `p-6` instead of standard `px-6 sm:px-8 lg:px-10 py-8`
File: `src/pages/Admin.tsx` line 42: `<main className="flex-1 overflow-y-auto p-6">`
Fix: Replace `p-6` with `px-6 sm:px-8 lg:px-10 py-8`

### BUG 5 — P2: SidebarLayout mobile trigger missing "Menu" label
File: `src/components/SidebarLayout.tsx` line 31 — the mobile trigger bar has `<SidebarTrigger />` but no "Menu" label text, while Index.tsx has it.
Fix: Add `<span className="ml-2 text-sm font-medium text-muted-foreground">Menu</span>` after the trigger.

### BUG 6 — P2: Dark mode sidebar contrast — sidebar bg vs content bg gap too small
In dark mode:
- `--rk-sidebar-background: 220 14% 5%` (very dark, nearly black)
- `--rk-background: 220 14% 8%` (only 3% lightness difference)
- The content area sits inside `bg-sidebar` wrapper with `p-2`, giving a very subtle visual separation.
- `--rk-sidebar-accent: 220 14% 10%` is used for active nav item bg — only 5% from sidebar bg.
Fix: Bump dark sidebar-accent from `220 14% 10%` to `220 14% 13%` for better active item contrast.

### BUG 7 — P1: AppHeader `hasSidebar` check using SidebarContext can return undefined (not null)
File: `src/components/AppHeader.tsx` line 93: `const hasSidebar = useContext(SidebarContext) !== null`
SidebarContext may return undefined when there is no provider, not null. This means pages without SidebarProvider incorrectly show the sidebar trigger.
Fix: `const hasSidebar = useContext(SidebarContext) != null` (loose inequality catches both null and undefined).

**Risk 3:** TaskSection cards are conditionally rendered — missing one in the list would leave an inconsistency.

- `teamName: string` — controlled input for the team name edit card (Settings tab)

### New functions to add

#### handleLeaveTeam()
```
- Confirm via AlertDialog (controlled with open state: leaveDialogOpen)
- Delete from team_members where user_id = user.id AND team_id = team.id
- On success: toast.success("You have left the team"), navigate("/")
- On error: toast.error("Failed to leave team")
- setIsLeavingTeam during operation
```

#### handleSaveTeamName()
```
- (supabase as any).from("teams").update({ name: teamName }).eq("id", team.id)
- On success: toast.success("Team name updated"), refetch()
- On error: toast.error("Failed to update team name")
```

### Fixes applied
1. **Date null guards**: Both `format(new Date(inv.expires_at), ...)` and `format(new Date(member.joined_at), ...)` wrapped in: `inv.expires_at ? format(new Date(inv.expires_at), "MMM d, yyyy") : "—"` and same for `member.joined_at`

2. **Revoke invitation confirmation**: Replace the direct `onClick={() => handleRevokeInvite(...)}` button with an `AlertDialog` wrapper (same pattern as member removal). State: `revokingDialogId: string | null` to control which invitation's dialog is open.

3. **Self-leave button**: In the page header, after the team name span, add a `Button variant="outline"` with `text-destructive` class, `LogOut` icon, "Leave team" label. Visible when `!canManage` (i.e., role === "member") — owners cannot leave. Triggers `leaveDialogOpen` state.

### AlertDialog state for self-leave

AlertDialog renders based on `revokeDialogTarget !== null`. Confirm calls `handleRevokeInvite(revokeDialogTarget.id, revokeDialogTarget.email)`.

# Plan: Direct Call & Email Contact Strip

## Context
The mockup spec adds a "Direct Call strip" to each candidate's expanded view — showing the phone number and email address as visible text with labeled action buttons (Call Now, Schedule, Email). Currently Rankora only has small icon buttons in the collapsed row with tooltips; the numbers are never visually displayed and there's no "Schedule" action. The goal is to add a structured contact card inside the expanded detail so recruiters can reach candidates without leaving the app.

## What Already Exists (reuse as-is)
- **Phone icon button** (`CandidateRow.tsx` lines 186–211) — copies on desktop, `tel:` on mobile. Keep in collapsed row.
- **Email icon + `EmailOutreachDialog`** (`src/components/candidate/EmailOutreachDialog.tsx`) — fully working templated email dialog. Reuse directly.
- **`outreach_log` DB table** — already has `type` column that accepts any string. Use `'call_now'` and `'call_scheduled'` without schema changes.
- **`send-candidate-email` edge function** — email sending already works, no changes.

## What to Add

### 1. New component: `src/components/candidate/ContactStrip.tsx`
A card shown in the expanded detail (after overview card, before Strengths & Gaps) with:

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  📧 rachita@email.com   [Email →]    📞 +91-9876543210  [Call Now]  [Schedule]  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Email section** (only if `candidate_email` exists):
- Show email address as text
- "Email" button → opens existing `EmailOutreachDialog` (state lifted to `ContactStrip`)

**Phone section** (only if `candidate_phone` exists):
- Show phone number as text
- "Call Now" button → `<a href="tel:...">` link + log to `outreach_log` (type: `'call_now'`)
- "Schedule" button → copies number to clipboard + logs to `outreach_log` (type: `'call_scheduled'`) + toast: "Call scheduled — phone number copied"

**If neither contact exists:** render nothing (return null).

**Call logging** (both Call Now + Schedule):
```typescript
await supabase.from("outreach_log" as any).insert({
  resume_id, job_id, type: 'call_now' | 'call_scheduled',
  to_address: candidate_phone, status: 'logged', user_id: session?.user.id
});
```
Use `(supabase as any)` cast (same pattern as seo_settings — not in auto-generated types).

**Props:**
```typescript
interface ContactStripProps {
  candidateEmail?: string | null;
  candidatePhone?: string | null;
  candidateName: string;
  jobTitle: string;
  matchScore: number;
  resumeId: string;
  jobId: string;
}
```

### 2. Modify `src/components/CandidateRow.tsx`
- Import `ContactStrip`
- In expanded detail, insert `<ContactStrip>` **after** the overview card and **before** `<StrengthsGapsPanel>`:
  ```tsx
  {/* Contact Strip */}
  <ContactStrip
    candidateEmail={r.candidate_email}
    candidatePhone={r.candidate_phone}
    candidateName={r.candidate_name || formatCandidateName(r.filename)}
    jobTitle={jobTitle || "Open Position"}
    matchScore={Math.round(r.overall_score ?? 0)}
    resumeId={r.resume_id}
    jobId={r.id}
  />
  ```
- Remove `EmailOutreachDialog` import + state + render from `CandidateRow` (moved into `ContactStrip`)
- The existing phone/email icon buttons in the **collapsed row** stay unchanged

| `src/components/CandidateRow.tsx` | Add ContactStrip, remove EmailOutreachDialog (moved inside ContactStrip) |

# Full App Audit — Issues & Fixes

## Context

Comprehensive audit of the entire Rankora app flow: login → job creation → JD analysis → file upload → AI ranking → results display. Goal: ensure every function works, remove dead code, fix ranking accuracy bugs, and make the customer experience smooth.

---

## Issues Table

| # | Severity | Component | Issue | Fix |
|---|----------|-----------|-------|-----|
| 1 | **CRITICAL** | `rank-resumes` L428-433, L470, L562-567 | `.single()` throws on zero rows when checking existing results — crashes entire batch | Replace all 3 with `.maybeSingle()` |
| 2 | **CRITICAL** | `rank-resumes` L550 | Hard fit cap uses `missingSkills` (must-haves + tools) instead of only critical must-haves — good candidates falsely capped at 49 | Filter to only `critical_must_haves` before checking hard_fit |
| 3 | **HIGH** | `rank-resumes` L470 | `.single()` inside catch block — if it throws, error propagates out of catch, crashing the entire batch of 5 resumes | Wrap fallback result insert in inner try-catch |
| 4 | **HIGH** | `Index.tsx` L186-195 | `handleDeleteJob()` deletes DB records but NOT storage files — orphaned files consume quota forever | Fetch `file_path` values from resumes, call `supabase.storage.from("resumes").remove()` before DB delete |
| 5 | **HIGH** | `JobDetailView` L47+89 | `UUID_REGEX` defined twice — top-level constant shadowed by component-level redefinition | Remove line 47 (top-level unused duplicate) |
| 6 | **MEDIUM** | `JobDetailView` L179 | `copyInboxEmail()` function defined but never called — inline copy exists at L682 | Remove the dead function |
| 7 | **MEDIUM** | `Index.tsx` L204-207 | `requireAuth` callback defined but never used anywhere | Remove dead code |
| 8 | **MEDIUM** | `CandidateRow` L298 | `r.experience` checked with `&&` but not `Array.isArray()` — crashes if malformed (string/object instead of array) | Add `Array.isArray(r.experience)` and `Array.isArray(r.education)` guards |
| 9 | **MEDIUM** | `CandidateRow` L378 | `category_scores_json` entries iterated without type guard on `data.score` — crashes if LLM returns malformed structure | Add `const score = (data as any)?.score ?? 0` |
| 10 | **LOW** | `JobDetailView` L6 | `Textarea` imported but never used | Remove unused import |
| 11 | **LOW** | `JobDetailView` L20 | `Zap` icon imported but never used (was used by old "AI analyzed" badge, now replaced by JdQualityBadge) | Remove from import |

---

## Files to Modify

### 1. `supabase/functions/rank-resumes/index.ts`

**Fix #1 — Replace `.single()` with `.maybeSingle()` (3 locations):**
- Line 433: `...eq("resume_id", rid).limit(1).single()` → `.maybeSingle()`
- Line 470: `...eq("resume_id", rid).limit(1).single()` → `.maybeSingle()`
- Line 567: `...eq("resume_id", resume.id).limit(1).single()` → `.maybeSingle()`

**Fix #2 — Heuristic hard_fit should only check critical must-haves, not tools (lines 526-550):**

Currently `missingSkills` tracks both must-haves AND tools that aren't matched. Then `hardFit = missingSkills.length === 0` caps at 49 if ANY tool is missing — even though missing a tool isn't a disqualification.

```ts
// Current code tracks missingSkills from both must-haves AND tools loops (lines 526-541)
// Then: let hardFit = missingSkills.length === 0;  ← WRONG, includes tools

// Fix: track missing critical must-haves separately
const missingCriticalMustHaves: string[] = [];
// In the must-haves loop (line 528-534), also push to missingCriticalMustHaves
// Then:
let hardFit = missingCriticalMustHaves.length === 0;  // Only critical, not tools
```
This matches Step 5 (deep scoring) which only checks `missing_critical_must_haves`.

**Fix #3 — Wrap fallback result insert in try-catch (line 465-483):**
```ts
} catch (err) {
  failCount++;
  console.error(`Error scoring resume ${rid}:`, err);
  await supabase.from("resumes").update({ status: "failed" }).eq("id", rid);
  try {
    const { data: existingResult } = await supabase.from("results")
      .select("id").eq("resume_id", rid).limit(1).maybeSingle();
    if (!existingResult) {
      await supabase.from("results").insert({ ... } as any);
    }
  } catch (insertErr) {
    console.error(`Failed to insert error result for ${rid}:`, insertErr);
  }
}
```

### 2. `src/pages/Index.tsx`

**Fix #4 — Storage cleanup on job delete (line 186-195):**
```ts
const handleDeleteJob = async (jobId: string) => {
  try {
    // Fetch file paths before deleting
    const { data: resumes } = await supabase
      .from("resumes")
      .select("file_path")
      .eq("job_id", jobId);
    const filePaths = (resumes || [])
      .map(r => r.file_path)
      .filter(Boolean) as string[];
    if (filePaths.length > 0) {
      await supabase.storage.from("resumes").remove(filePaths);
    }
    // Then delete DB records
    await supabase.from("resumes").delete().eq("job_id", jobId);
    await supabase.from("jobs").delete().eq("id", jobId);
    // ... rest unchanged
  }
};
```

**Fix #7 — Remove unused `requireAuth`:**
Delete lines 204-207.

Delete line 47 (`const UUID_REGEX = ...` outside component).

**Fix #6 — Remove unused `copyInboxEmail`:**
Delete the function at line 179-183.

**Fix #10 — Remove unused `Textarea` import:**
Remove `Textarea` from line 6.

**Fix #11 — Remove unused `Zap` import:**
Remove `Zap` from the lucide-react import line.

### 4. `src/components/CandidateRow.tsx`

**Fix #8 — Add Array.isArray guards:**
```ts
// Experience card
{Array.isArray(r.experience) && r.experience.length > 0 ? (

// Education card
{Array.isArray(r.education) && r.education.length > 0 ? (
```

**Fix #9 — Safe category_scores_json iteration:**
```ts
{Object.entries(r.category_scores_json).map(([skill, data]) => {
  const score = typeof (data as any)?.score === 'number' ? (data as any).score : 0;
  const lvl = getSkillLevel(score);
```

# ConvertScan UI Modernization Plan

## Context
The current UI looks dated — "old type and server website type." The goal is to make every page feel **clean, airy, energetic, and premium** — matching modern SaaS standards like the reference screenshot (clean data table with color-coded score badges, filter bar, generous spacing, subtle shadows).

No functionality changes. Pure visual upgrade using existing stack (Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Recharts, Lucide).

---

*Every component inherits these changes immediately.*

### `src/index.css`
- `--radius`: `0.5rem` → `0.75rem` (ripples through every Card, Button, Input, Dialog)
- `--background`: `0 0% 98.4%` → `0 0% 99%` (whiter background = cards pop more)
- `--border`: `220 13% 91%` → `220 13% 93%` (subtler card edges)
- `--topbar-height`: `56px` → `60px`
